  "short_description": {
    "id": "Loong adalah naga dalam mitologi Tiongkok, lazim digambarkan menyerupai ular dengan empat kaki.",
    "en": "Loong belongs to Chinese mythology and commonly has a serpentine shape with four legs.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "loong-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Loong adalah naga dalam mitologi Tiongkok, lazim digambarkan menyerupai ular dengan empat kaki. Naga menjadi lambang kekuasaan kaisar dalam sejarah Tiongkok.",
      "en": "Loong belongs to Chinese mythology and commonly has a serpentine shape with four legs. The dragon served as an emblem of imperial authority in Chinese history.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "loong-c01",
        "loong-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Bentuk naga melingkar juga ditemukan pada amulet giok zaman Shang. Sebagian benda yang dahulu disebut tulang naga ternyata fosil mamalia. Wang Fu mencatat tradisi sembilan kemiripan anatomi naga dengan hewan lain. Daftar ciri naga memiliki varian; Doré mencatat kepala buaya dan tanduk rusa. Naga dalam seni sering mengejar atau memperebutkan mutiara menyala. Kebanyakan naga Tiongkok tidak bersayap; penerbangannya dijelaskan sebagai kekuatan gaib. Kepercayaan populer menghubungkan naga dengan sungai, laut, dan pengaturan hujan. Saat kekeringan atau banjir, masyarakat melakukan ritual untuk memohon bantuan raja naga setempat. Sebuah legenda mengisahkan Kaisar Kuning naik ke langit setelah menjadi naga. Museum Norton menjelaskan naga sebagai penguasa langit yang dimohon mendatangkan hujan dalam ritual. Pada jubah istana, naga merujuk pada mandat kaisar untuk memerintah dari Takhta Naga. Museum V&A menafsirkan mutiara menyala dalam motif naga sebagai kebijaksanaan, pencerahan, dan hakikat spiritual. Pola naga pada keramik ekspor Tiongkok mengilhami keramik cetak biru-putih Inggris abad kesembilan belas.",
      "en": "Coiled dragon forms occur on jade amulets from the Shang period. Scientific examination identifies many objects called dragon bones as mammal fossils. Wang Fu recorded a tradition of nine animal resemblances in dragon anatomy. Dragon anatomy varies across accounts; Doré lists a crocodile head and deer antlers. Art often shows dragons pursuing or contesting a flaming pearl. Most Chinese dragons lack wings, with flight attributed to supernatural power. Popular belief connects dragons with rivers, seas, and the distribution of rain. Communities conducted rituals to seek help from local dragon kings during droughts or floods. One legend describes the Yellow Emperor ascending to heaven after becoming a dragon. The Norton Museum describes dragons as heavenly rulers petitioned for rain through ritual. On court robes, dragon imagery refers to the emperor’s mandate to rule from the Dragon Throne. The V&A interprets the flaming pearl in dragon imagery as wisdom, enlightenment, and spiritual essence. Dragon patterns on Chinese export ceramics inspired British printed blue-and-white wares in the nineteenth century.",

      "author": null,

    {

      "url": "https://www.vam.ac.uk/blog/blue-and-white/here-be-dragons",
      "title": "'Here be Dragons' • V&A Blog",
      "author": "Rebecca Wallis",
      "publisher": "Victoria and Albert Museum",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "museum-or-archive",
      "accessed": "2026-10-01"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "loong-c01",
      "source_id": "loong-s1",
      "quote": "The Chinese dragon, known as long or loong (/lʊŋ/ LUUNG) is a legendary creature in Chinese mythology, Chinese folklore, and Chinese culture generally. Chinese dragons have many animal-like forms, such as turtles and fish, but are most commonly depicted as snake-like with four legs.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Loong adalah naga dalam mitologi Tiongkok, lazim digambarkan menyerupai ular dengan empat kaki.",
        "en": "Loong belongs to Chinese mythology and commonly has a serpentine shape with four legs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c02",
      "source_id": "loong-s1",
      "quote": "Historically, the Chinese dragon was associated with the emperor of China and used as a symbol to represent imperial power. Liu Bang, the founder of the Han dynasty, claimed that he was conceived after his mother dreamt of a dragon. During the Tang dynasty, emperors wore robes with dragon motif as an imperial symbol, and high officials might also be presented with dragon robes.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga menjadi lambang kekuasaan kaisar dalam sejarah Tiongkok.",
        "en": "The dragon served as an emblem of imperial authority in Chinese history."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c03",
      "source_id": "loong-s1",
      "quote": "The coiled dragon or snake form played an important role in early Chinese culture. The character for \"dragon\" in the earliest Chinese writing has a similar coiled form, as do later jade dragon amulets from the Shang period.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk naga melingkar juga ditemukan pada amulet giok zaman Shang.",
        "en": "Coiled dragon forms occur on jade amulets from the Shang period."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c04",
      "source_id": "loong-s1",
      "quote": "Ancient Chinese referred to unearthed fossil bones as \"dragon bones\" and documented them as such. For example, Chang Qu in 300 BC documents the discovery of \"dragon bones\" in Sichuan. \"Dragon bones\" have been used both historically and in modern times in traditional Chinese medicine.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian benda yang dahulu disebut tulang naga ternyata fosil mamalia.",
        "en": "Scientific examination identifies many objects called dragon bones as mammal fossils."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c05",
      "source_id": "loong-s1",
      "quote": "From its origins as totems or the stylized depiction of natural creatures, the Chinese dragon evolved to become a mythical animal. The Han dynasty scholar Wang Fu recorded Chinese myths that long dragons had nine anatomical resemblances.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 10",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wang Fu mencatat tradisi sembilan kemiripan anatomi naga dengan hewan lain.",
        "en": "Wang Fu recorded a tradition of nine animal resemblances in dragon anatomy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c06",
      "source_id": "loong-s1",
      "quote": "Further sources give variant lists of the nine animal resemblances. Sinologist Henri Doré lists these characteristics of an authentic dragon: \"The antlers of a deer. The head of a crocodile. A demon's eyes. The neck of a snake. A tortoise's viscera. A hawk's claws. The palms of a tiger. A cow's ears. And it hears through its horns, its ears being deprived of all power of hearing\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 12",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Daftar ciri naga memiliki varian; Doré mencatat kepala buaya dan tanduk rusa.",
        "en": "Dragon anatomy varies across accounts; Doré lists a crocodile head and deer antlers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c07",
      "source_id": "loong-s1",
      "quote": "Many pictures of Chinese dragons show a flaming pearl under their chin or in their claws. The pearl is associated with spiritual energy, wisdom, prosperity, power, immortality, thunder, or the moon. Chinese art often depicts a pair of dragons chasing or fighting over a flaming pearl.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 14",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga dalam seni sering mengejar atau memperebutkan mutiara menyala.",
        "en": "Art often shows dragons pursuing or contesting a flaming pearl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c08",
      "source_id": "loong-s1",
      "quote": "Chinese dragons are occasionally depicted with bat-like wings growing out of the front limbs, but most do not have wings, as their ability to fly (and control rain/water, etc.) is mystical and not seen as a result of their physical attributes.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 15",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kebanyakan naga Tiongkok tidak bersayap; penerbangannya dijelaskan sebagai kekuatan gaib.",
        "en": "Most Chinese dragons lack wings, with flight attributed to supernatural power."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c09",
      "source_id": "loong-s1",
      "quote": "Chinese dragons are strongly associated with water and weather in popular religion. They are believed to be the rulers of moving bodies of water, such as waterfalls, rivers, or seas. The Dragon god is the dispenser of rain as well as the zoomorphic representation of yang, the masculine power of generation.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 21",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepercayaan populer menghubungkan naga dengan sungai, laut, dan pengaturan hujan.",
        "en": "Popular belief connects dragons with rivers, seas, and the distribution of rain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c10",
      "source_id": "loong-s1",
      "quote": "Because of this association, they are seen as \"in charge\" of water-related weather phenomena. In premodern times, many Chinese villages (especially those close to rivers and seas) had temples dedicated to their local \"dragon king\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 23",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saat kekeringan atau banjir, masyarakat melakukan ritual untuk memohon bantuan raja naga setempat.",
        "en": "Communities conducted rituals to seek help from local dragon kings during droughts or floods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c11",
      "source_id": "loong-s1",
      "quote": "According to Chinese legend, both Chinese primogenitors, the earliest Door and the Yellow Emperor, were closely related to the dragon. At the end of his reign, the first legendary ruler, the Yellow Emperor, was said to have been immortalized into a dragon that resembled his emblem, and ascended to Heaven.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 26",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah legenda mengisahkan Kaisar Kuning naik ke langit setelah menjadi naga.",
        "en": "One legend describes the Yellow Emperor ascending to heaven after becoming a dragon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c12",
      "source_id": "loong-s2",
      "quote": "Each work depicts dragons, the heavenly sovereigns, petitioned to bring rain in ritual ceremonies.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Museum Norton menjelaskan naga sebagai penguasa langit yang dimohon mendatangkan hujan dalam ritual.",
        "en": "The Norton Museum describes dragons as heavenly rulers petitioned for rain through ritual."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c13",
      "source_id": "loong-s2",
      "quote": "The presence of dragons on the robes refers to the emperor holding the Mandate of Heaven to rule from the Dragon Throne.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada jubah istana, naga merujuk pada mandat kaisar untuk memerintah dari Takhta Naga.",
        "en": "On court robes, dragon imagery refers to the emperor’s mandate to rule from the Dragon Throne."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c14",
      "source_id": "loong-s3",
      "quote": "It is highly unlikely the decorator of this object knew that the image depicts the Chinese legend of the Dragon pursuing the ‘Flaming Pearl’ representing wisdom, enlightenment and spiritual essence.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Museum V&A menafsirkan mutiara menyala dalam motif naga sebagai kebijaksanaan, pencerahan, dan hakikat spiritual.",
        "en": "The V&A interprets the flaming pearl in dragon imagery as wisdom, enlightenment, and spiritual essence."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loong-c15",
      "source_id": "loong-s3",
      "quote": "Popularity of these export wares continued into the 19th century when British production of printed blue and white ceramics drew inspiration from the Asian patterns.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pola naga pada keramik ekspor Tiongkok mengilhami keramik cetak biru-putih Inggris abad kesembilan belas.",
        "en": "Dragon patterns on Chinese export ceramics inspired British printed blue-and-white wares in the nineteenth century."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

}

      "source_id": "nuwa-s1",
      "quote": "Nüwa, also read Nügua, is a mother goddess, culture hero, and/or member of the Three Sovereigns of Chinese mythology. She is a goddess in Chinese folk religion, Chinese Buddhism, Confucianism and Taoism. She is credited with creating humanity and repairing the Pillar of Heaven.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nüwa adalah dewi pencipta manusia dalam mitologi Tiongkok.",
        "en": "In Chinese mythology, Nüwa is a goddess associated with humanity's creation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuwa-c02",
      "source_id": "nuwa-s1",
      "quote": "As creator of mankind, she molded humans individually by hand with yellow clay. In other stories where she fulfills this role, she only created nobles and/or the rich out of yellow soil. The stories vary on the other details about humanity's creation, but it was a tradition commonly believed in ancient China that she created commoners from brown mud.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

        "en": "A creation account describes her shaping people from yellow clay."

    },

      "source_id": "nuwa-s1",
      "quote": "As creator of mankind, she molded humans individually by hand with yellow clay. In other stories where she fulfills this role, she only created nobles and/or the rich out of yellow soil. The stories vary on the other details about humanity's creation, but it was a tradition commonly believed in ancient China that she created commoners from brown mud.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi lain membedakan manusia yang dibentuk tangan dan manusia yang dibuat melalui tali berlumpur.",
        "en": "Another version distinguishes hand-shaped people from those made using a muddy cord."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nuwa-c04",
      "source_id": "nuwa-s1",
      "quote": "In the Huainanzi, there is a description of a great battle between deities that broke the pillars supporting Heaven and caused great devastation.   "short_description": {
    "id": "Jade Emperor atau Yudi merupakan tokoh ilahi dalam mitos dan agama rakyat Tiongkok.",
    "en": "The Jade Emperor, also called Yudi, is a divine figure in Chinese myth and folk religion.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "jade-emperor-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Jade Emperor atau Yudi merupakan tokoh ilahi dalam mitos dan agama rakyat Tiongkok. Dalam teologi Tao, ia ditempatkan sebagai pembantu Yuanshi Tianzun.",
      "en": "The Jade Emperor, also called Yudi, is a divine figure in Chinese myth and folk religion. Taoist theology places him in an assisting role to Yuanshi Tianzun.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jade-emperor-c01",
        "jade-emperor-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kosmologi Buddhis Tiongkok kadang menyamakannya dengan Śakra. Gelar yang dipakai antara lain Tiangong dan Yu Huang Shangdi. Kisah asal-usul menggambarkannya sebagai putra mahkota yang memancarkan cahaya saat lahir. Salah satu mitos menceritakan bagaimana ia menjadi penguasa para dewa di langit.",
      "en": "Chinese Buddhist cosmology sometimes equates him with Śakra. His titles include Tiangong and Yu Huang Shangdi. An origin tale portrays him as a crown prince whose birth emits extraordinary light. One myth recounts his becoming ruler of the celestial deities.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jade-emperor-c03",
        "jade-emperor-c04",
        "jade-emperor-c05",
        "jade-emperor-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

    {

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "jade-emperor-c01",
      "source_id": "jade-emperor-s1",
      "quote": "In the myths and folk religion of Chinese culture, the Jade Emperor or Yudi is one of the representations of the primordial god.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jade Emperor atau Yudi merupakan tokoh ilahi dalam mitos dan agama rakyat Tiongkok.",
        "en": "The Jade Emperor, also called Yudi, is a divine figure in Chinese myth and folk religion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jade-emperor-c02",
      "source_id": "jade-emperor-s1",
      "quote": "In Taoist theology, he is the assistant of Yuanshi Tianzun, who is one of the Three Pure Ones, the three primordial emanations of the Tao. However, some Taoists in history were skeptical of his benevolence because his buildings and infrastructure in heaven and earth were sometimes seen as interfering with the many natural laws or dao.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam teologi Tao, ia ditempatkan sebagai pembantu Yuanshi Tianzun.",
        "en": "Taoist theology places him in an assisting role to Yuanshi Tianzun."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jade-emperor-c03",
      "source_id": "jade-emperor-s1",
      "quote": "He is often identified with Śakra in Chinese Buddhist cosmology and identified with Yu the Great in Chinese mythology.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kosmologi Buddhis Tiongkok kadang menyamakannya dengan Śakra.",
        "en": "Chinese Buddhist cosmology sometimes equates him with Śakra."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jade-emperor-c04",
      "source_id": "jade-emperor-s1",
      "quote": "The Jade Emperor is known by many names, including Yu; Heavenly Grandfather (Chinese: 天公, romanized: Tiāngōng), which originally meant \"Heavenly Duke\" (and which is used by commoners); the Jade Lord; the Highest Emperor; and Great Emperor of Jade (玉皇上帝, Yu Huang Shangdi, or 玉皇大帝, Yu Huang Dadi).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gelar yang dipakai antara lain Tiangong dan Yu Huang Shangdi.",
        "en": "His titles include Tiangong and Yu Huang Shangdi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jade-emperor-c05",
      "source_id": "jade-emperor-s1",
      "quote": "It was said that Jade Emperor was originally the crown prince of the kingdom of Pure Felicity and Majestic Heavenly Lights and Ornaments. At birth, he emitted a wondrous light that filled the entire kingdom. When he was young, he was kind, intelligent and wise.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah asal-usul menggambarkannya sebagai putra mahkota yang memancarkan cahaya saat lahir.",
        "en": "An origin tale portrays him as a crown prince whose birth emits extraordinary light."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jade-emperor-c06",
      "source_id": "jade-emperor-s1",
      "quote": "One of the myths describes how the Jade Emperor became the monarch of all the deities in heaven. It is one of the few myths in which the Jade Emperor shows his power.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu mitos menceritakan bagaimana ia menjadi penguasa para dewa di langit.",
        "en": "One myth recounts his becoming ruler of the celestial deities."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Fenghuang adalah burung mitologis yang dikenal dalam tradisi kawasan budaya Tiongkok. Fenghuang dianggap berkuasa atas burung lainnya dan sering dipasangkan dengan naga.",
      "en": "Fenghuang are mythic birds found across traditions influenced by Chinese culture. Fenghuang hold supremacy among birds and are often paired with dragons.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fenghuang-c01",
        "fenghuang-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebutan phoenix Tiongkok tidak berarti kisahnya sama dengan phoenix Barat. Erya menggambarkannya sebagai gabungan ciri berbagai hewan, termasuk paruh ayam dan leher ular. Kepala, mata, dan punggungnya dihubungkan dengan langit, matahari, dan bulan. Sebuah tradisi menempatkan kediamannya di Gunung Danzuan di pegunungan selatan.",
      "en": "Calling it a Chinese phoenix does not imply the same mythology as the Western phoenix. The Erya describes a composite anatomy including a rooster's beak and a snake's neck.       "quote": "Fenghuang (/fʌŋˈwɑːŋ/ fung-HWAHNG) are mythological birds featuring in traditions throughout the Sinosphere.",

    {
      "id": "fenghuang-c03",
      "source_id": "fenghuang-s1",
      "quote": "In the West, they are commonly called Chinese phoenixes, although mythological similarities with the Western/Persian phoenix are superficial.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebutan phoenix Tiongkok tidak berarti kisahnya sama dengan phoenix Barat.",
        "en": "Calling it a Chinese phoenix does not imply the same mythology as the Western phoenix."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fenghuang-c04",
      "source_id": "fenghuang-s1",
      "quote": "A common depiction of fenghuang was of it attacking snakes with its talons and its wings spread. According to the Erya's chapter 17 Shiniao, fenghuang is made up of the beak of a rooster, the face of a swallow, the forehead of a fowl, the neck of a snake, the breast of a goose, the back of a tortoise, the hindquarters of a stag and the tail of a fish.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Erya menggambarkannya sebagai gabungan ciri berbagai hewan, termasuk paruh ayam dan leher ular.",
        "en": "The Erya describes a composite anatomy including a rooster's beak and a snake's neck."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fenghuang-c05",
      "source_id": "fenghuang-s1",
      "quote": "The fenghuang's body symbolizes the celestial bodies: the head is the sky, the eyes are the sun, the back is the moon, the wings are the wind, the feet are the earth, and the tail is the planets. The fenghuang is said to have originated in the sun. Its body contains the five fundamental colors: black, white, red, yellow, and green or blue.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepala, mata, dan punggungnya dihubungkan dengan langit, matahari, dan bulan.",
        "en": "Its head, eyes, and back are associated with the sky, sun, and moon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fenghuang-c06",
      "source_id": "fenghuang-s1",
      "quote": "Chinese tradition cites it as living atop Mount Danzuan in the southern mountains.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah tradisi menempatkan kediamannya di Gunung Danzuan di pegunungan selatan.",

      }

  },

    {
      "id": "Pangu adalah tokoh penciptaan purba dalam mitologi Tiongkok yang memisahkan langit dan bumi. Xu Zheng dianggap sebagai penulis awal yang mencatat mitos Pangu.",
      "en": "Pangu is a primordial creation figure in Chinese mythology who separates heaven from earth. Xu Zheng is regarded as an early writer who recorded the Pangu myth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pangu-c01",
        "pangu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah telur kosmis, Pangu muncul setelah yin dan yang mencapai keseimbangan. Pangu lazim digambarkan sebagai raksasa berbulu dengan tanduk di kepala. Sebagian versi menyebut bantuan Empat Makhluk Bertuah dalam penciptaan dunia. Sesudah kematiannya, napas menjadi angin dan awan, suara menjadi guntur, dan mata menjadi matahari serta bulan.",
      "en": "In the cosmic-egg tale, Pangu emerges once yin and yang reach balance. Depictions commonly present Pangu as a hairy giant bearing horns. Some versions describe the Four Auspicious Beasts assisting in world creation. After his death, his breath becomes wind and clouds, his voice becomes thunder, and his eyes become sun and moon.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pangu-c03",
        "pangu-c04",
        "pangu-c05",
        "pangu-c06"

  ],
  "cultural_context": null,

      "id": "pangu-c01",
      "source_id": "pangu-s1",
      "quote": "Pangu or Pan Gu (also sometimes spelled Peng Gu and P’an-ku)\n(Chinese: 盤古; pinyin: Pángǔ ; PAN-koo) is a primordial being and creation figure in Chinese mythology and in Taoism. According to legend, Pangu separated heaven and earth, and his body later became geographic features such as mountains and flowing water.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pangu adalah tokoh penciptaan purba dalam mitologi Tiongkok yang memisahkan langit dan bumi.",
        "en": "Pangu is a primordial creation figure in Chinese mythology who separates heaven from earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pangu-c02",
      "source_id": "pangu-s1",
      "quote": "The earliest known writer to record the myth of Pangu is believed to have been Xu Zheng during the Three Kingdoms period. However, his name was found in a tomb predating the Three Kingdoms period.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xu Zheng dianggap sebagai penulis awal yang mencatat mitos Pangu.",
        "en": "Xu Zheng is regarded as an early writer who recorded the Pangu myth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pangu-c03",
      "source_id": "pangu-s1",
      "quote": "In the beginning, there was nothing and the universe was in a featureless, formless primordial state. This primordial state coalesced into a cosmic egg over the course of about 18,000 years. Within it, the perfectly opposed principles of yin and yang became balanced and Pangu emerged (or woke up) from the egg. Pangu inside the cosmic egg symbolizes Taiji.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah telur kosmis, Pangu muncul setelah yin dan yang mencapai keseimbangan.",
        "en": "In the cosmic-egg tale, Pangu emerges once yin and yang reach balance."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pangu-c04",
      "source_id": "pangu-s1",
      "quote": "Pangu inside the cosmic egg symbolizes Taiji. Pangu is usually depicted as a primitive, hairy giant with horns on his head. ",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pangu lazim digambarkan sebagai raksasa berbulu dengan tanduk di kepala.",
        "en": "Depictions commonly present Pangu as a hairy giant bearing horns."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pangu-c05",
      "source_id": "pangu-s1",
      "quote": "In some versions of the story, Pangu is aided in this task by the Four Auspicious Beasts (四靈獸). There are many different legends about Pangu's  creation of the world, with some saying that this task took 18,000 years, while others say that he lived for millions of years.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian versi menyebut bantuan Empat Makhluk Bertuah dalam penciptaan dunia.",
        "en": "Some versions describe the Four Auspicious Beasts assisting in world creation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pangu-c06",
      "source_id": "pangu-s1",
      "quote": "When Pangu died, his breath became the wind, mist and clouds; his voice, thunder; his left eye, the Sun; his right eye, the Moon; his head, the mountains and extremes of the world; his blood, rivers; his muscles, fertile land; his facial hair, the stars and Milky Way; his fur, bushes and forests; his bones, valuable minerals; his bone marrow, precious jewels; his sweat, rain; and the fle",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sesudah kematiannya, napas menjadi angin dan awan, suara menjadi guntur, dan mata menjadi matahari serta bulan.",
        "en": "After his death, his breath becomes wind and clouds, his voice becomes thunder, and his eyes become sun and moon."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Mazu adalah dewi laut dalam agama rakyat Tiongkok dan tradisi keagamaan terkait.",
    "en": "Mazu is a sea goddess in Chinese folk religion and related religious traditions.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mazu adalah dewi laut dalam agama rakyat Tiongkok dan tradisi keagamaan terkait. Ia dipahami sebagai Lin Moniang yang didewakan, seorang perempuan dari Fujian.",
      "en": "Mazu is a sea goddess in Chinese folk religion and related religious traditions. She is understood as the deified Lin Moniang, a woman from Fujian.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mazu-c01",
        "mazu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tradisi menggambarkannya melindungi pengikut yang berlayar melalui pertolongan ajaib. Perayaan kuilnya penting di Taiwan, terutama sekitar Dajia dan Beigang. Dalam keadaan darurat, nama Mazu dipercaya mendapat tanggapan lebih cepat daripada gelar resmi. Legenda mengenai kehidupan Lin Moniang sudah terbentuk secara luas pada abad kedua belas.",
      "en": "Tradition describes miraculous interventions protecting devotees at sea. Her temple celebrations are prominent in Taiwan, notably around Dajia and Beigang. During emergencies, invoking Mazu is believed to bring a quicker response than using formal titles. Legends of Lin Moniang's life were broadly established by the twelfth century.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mazu-c03",
        "mazu-c04",
        "mazu-c05",
        "mazu-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mazu-c01",
      "source_id": "mazu-s1",
      "quote": "Mazu or Matsu is a sea goddess in Chinese folk religion, Chinese Buddhism, Confucianism, and Taoism. She is also known by several other names and titles. Mazu is the deified form of Lin Moniang (Chinese: 林默娘; pinyin: Lín Mòniáng; Pe̍h-ōe-jī: Lîm Be̍k-niû / Lîm Bia̍k-niû / Lîm Be̍k-niô͘), a shamaness from Fujian who is said to have lived in the late 10th century.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mazu adalah dewi laut dalam agama rakyat Tiongkok dan tradisi keagamaan terkait.",
        "en": "Mazu is a sea goddess in Chinese folk religion and related religious traditions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mazu-c02",
      "source_id": "mazu-s1",
      "quote": "Mazu is the deified form of Lin Moniang (Chinese: 林默娘; pinyin: Lín Mòniáng; Pe̍h-ōe-jī: Lîm Be̍k-niû / Lîm Bia̍k-niû / Lîm Be̍k-niô͘), a shamaness from Fujian who is said to have lived in the late 10th century.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dipahami sebagai Lin Moniang yang didewakan, seorang perempuan dari Fujian.",
        "en": "She is understood as the deified Lin Moniang, a woman from Fujian."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mazu-c03",
      "source_id": "mazu-s1",
      "quote": "Her worship spread throughout China's coastal regions and overseas Chinese communities throughout Southeast Asia, where some Mazu temples are affiliated with famous Taiwanese temples. Traditionally, Mazu was believed to roam the seas, safeguarding her devotees through miraculous interventions.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi menggambarkannya melindungi pengikut yang berlayar melalui pertolongan ajaib.",
        "en": "Tradition describes miraculous interventions protecting devotees at sea."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mazu-c04",
      "source_id": "mazu-s1",
      "quote": "Mazu worship is popular in Taiwan because many early Chinese settlers in Taiwan were Hoklo people from Fujian. Her temple festival is a major event in Taiwan, with the largest celebrations occurring in and around her temples at Dajia and Beigang.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Perayaan kuilnya penting di Taiwan, terutama sekitar Dajia dan Beigang.",
        "en": "Her temple celebrations are prominent in Taiwan, notably around Dajia and Beigang."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mazu-c05",
      "source_id": "mazu-s1",
      "quote": "Although many of Mazu's temples honor her titles Tianhou and Tianfei, it became customary to never pray to her under those names during an emergency since it was believed that, hearing one of her formal titles, Mazu might feel obligated to groom and dress herself as properly befitting her station before receiving the petition.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam keadaan darurat, nama Mazu dipercaya mendapat tanggapan lebih cepat daripada gelar resmi.",
        "en": "During emergencies, invoking Mazu is believed to bring a quicker response than using formal titles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mazu-c06",
      "source_id": "mazu-s1",
      "quote": "The legends around Lin Moniang's life were broadly established by the 12th century.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda mengenai kehidupan Lin Moniang sudah terbentuk secara luas pada abad kedua belas.",
        "en": "Legends of Lin Moniang's life were broadly established by the twelfth century."
      }
    }
  ],

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Qilin adalah makhluk campuran berkuku dalam mitologi Tiongkok. Kemunculannya dikaitkan dengan kedatangan atau kematian seorang bijak maupun penguasa terkemuka.",
      "en": "The qilin is a hoofed composite creature in Chinese mythology. Its appearance is linked to the arrival or death of a sage or distinguished ruler.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "qilin-c01",
        "qilin-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Syair dalam Kitab Nyanyian memuat penyebutan awal lin mitologis. Catatan Musim Semi dan Gugur menyebut penangkapan lin pada tahun keempat belas Adipati Ai dari Lu. Shuowen Jiezi membedakan qi sebagai jantan dan lin sebagai betina. Pada zaman Ming, citra qilin dihubungkan dengan jerapah yang dibawa setelah pelayaran Zheng He.",
      "en": "A poem in the Classic of Poetry provides an early mention of the mythical lin. The Spring and Autumn Annals record a lin's capture in Duke Ai of Lu's fourteenth year. The Shuowen Jiezi assigns qi to the male and lin to the female. During the Ming period, qilin imagery was associated with giraffes brought following Zheng He's voyages.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "qilin-c03",
        "qilin-c04",
        "qilin-c05",
        "qilin-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "qilin-c01",
      "source_id": "qilin-s1",
      "quote": "The qilin (English: /tʃiˈlɪn/ chee-LIN; Chinese: 麒麟; pinyin: qílín) is a legendary hooved chimerical creature that appears in Chinese mythology, and is said to appear with the imminent arrival or death of a sage or illustrious ruler. Qilin are a specific type of the lin mythological family of one-horned beasts.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Qilin adalah makhluk campuran berkuku dalam mitologi Tiongkok.",
        "en": "The qilin is a hoofed composite creature in Chinese mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "qilin-c02",
      "source_id": "qilin-s1",
      "quote": "The qilin (English: /tʃiˈlɪn/ chee-LIN; Chinese: 麒麟; pinyin: qílín) is a legendary hooved chimerical creature that appears in Chinese mythology, and is said to appear with the imminent arrival or death of a sage or illustrious ruler. Qilin are a specific type of the lin mythological family of one-horned beasts.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kemunculannya dikaitkan dengan kedatangan atau kematian seorang bijak maupun penguasa terkemuka.",
        "en": "Its appearance is linked to the arrival or death of a sage or distinguished ruler."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "qilin-c03",
      "source_id": "qilin-s1",
      "quote": "The earliest mention of the mythical qilin is in the poem 麟之趾; Lín zhī zhǐ; 'Feet of the Lin' included in the Classic of Poetry (11th – 7th c. BCE).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Syair dalam Kitab Nyanyian memuat penyebutan awal lin mitologis.",
        "en": "A poem in the Classic of Poetry provides an early mention of the mythical lin."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "qilin-c04",
      "source_id": "qilin-s1",
      "quote": "Spring and Autumn Annals mentioned that a lin (麟) was captured in the 14th year of Duke Ai of Lu (魯哀公) (481 BCE); Zuo Zhuan credited Confucius with identifying the lin as such.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan Musim Semi dan Gugur menyebut penangkapan lin pada tahun keempat belas Adipati Ai dari Lu.",
        "en": "The Spring and Autumn Annals record a lin's capture in Duke Ai of Lu's fourteenth year."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "qilin-c05",
      "source_id": "qilin-s1",
      "quote": "The bisyllabic form qilin (麒麟 ~ 騏驎), which carries the same generic meaning as lin alone, is attested in works dated to the Warring States period (475–221 BCE). Qi denotes the male and lin denotes the female according to Shuowen Jiezi.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shuowen Jiezi membedakan qi sebagai jantan dan lin sebagai betina.",
        "en": "The Shuowen Jiezi assigns qi to the male and lin to the female."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "qilin-c06",
      "source_id": "qilin-s1",
      "quote": "The legendary image of the qilin became associated with the image of the giraffe in the Ming dynasty. The identification of the qilin with giraffes began after Zheng He's 15th-century voyage to East Africa (landing, among other places, Ma-lin (麻林): Malindi (in modern-day Kenya)).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada zaman Ming, citra qilin dihubungkan dengan jerapah yang dibawa setelah pelayaran Zheng He.",
        "en": "During the Ming period, qilin imagery was associated with giraffes brought following Zheng He's voyages."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Zhuque atau Burung Merah adalah salah satu Empat Lambang rasi Tiongkok. Dalam sistem lima unsur, ia mewakili api, selatan, dan musim panas.",
      "en": "Zhuque, the Vermilion Bird, belongs to the Four Symbols of Chinese constellations. In the five-phase system, it corresponds to fire, south, and summer.",

        "zhuque-c01",
        "zhuque-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Burung Merah merupakan roh rasi yang berbeda dari fenghuang. Tujuh kedudukan bulan dikelompokkan dalam wilayah rasi Burung Merah. Penggambarannya menekankan keanggunan dan pilihan makanan serta tempat bertengger yang selektif.",

  "region": {
    "value": "east-asia",
    "claim_ids": [
      "black-tortoise-c01"
    ]
  },
  "countries": null,
  "era": null,
  "habitats": [],
  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Black Tortoise atau Xuanwu adalah lambang rasi Tiongkok berupa kura-kura yang dililit ular.",
    "en": "Black Tortoise, or Xuanwu, is a Chinese constellation symbol showing a turtle intertwined with a snake.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "black-tortoise-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Black Tortoise atau Xuanwu adalah lambang rasi Tiongkok berupa kura-kura yang dililit ular. Penjelasan Xu Shen mengaitkan kata wu dengan kesatria dalam dialek Jianghuai.",
      "en": "Black Tortoise, or Xuanwu, is a Chinese constellation symbol showing a turtle intertwined with a snake. Xu Shen's explanation links wu to the word for knight in the Jianghuai dialect.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "black-tortoise-c01",
        "black-tortoise-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Xuanwu dikaitkan dengan arah utara. Kura-kura dan ular yang berhibernasi mendasari hubungannya dengan musim dingin. Di Jepang, Genbu dipercaya menjaga sisi utara Kyoto. Sebuah legenda pasangan kura-kura dan ular menjelaskan bentuk mereka yang saling melilit.",
      "en": "Xuanwu is associated with the northern direction. The winter association draws on the hibernation of turtles and snakes. In Japan, Genbu is believed to guard Kyoto's northern side. A mating legend about turtles and snakes explains their intertwined portrayal.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "black-tortoise-c03",
        "black-tortoise-c04",
        "black-tortoise-c05",
        "black-tortoise-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],
  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,
  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "black-tortoise-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Turtle-Snake",
      "title": "Black Turtle-Snake",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-01"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "black-tortoise-c01",
      "source_id": "black-tortoise-s1",
      "quote": "The Black Turtle-Snake (Chinese: 玄武; pinyin: Xuánwǔ) is one of the Four Symbols of the Chinese constellations. It is usually depicted as a tortoise or turtle, intertwined with a snake.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Black Tortoise atau Xuanwu adalah lambang rasi Tiongkok berupa kura-kura yang dililit ular.",
        "en": "Black Tortoise, or Xuanwu, is a Chinese constellation symbol showing a turtle intertwined with a snake."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "black-tortoise-c02",
      "source_id": "black-tortoise-s1",
      "quote": "According to Eastern Han philologist Xu Shen, the character wu (武) meant \"knight\" (士; shì ) in the Jianghuai dialect.  So the English translation Black ~ Dark ~ Mysterious Warrior is a more faithful translation.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penjelasan Xu Shen mengaitkan kata wu dengan kesatria dalam dialek Jianghuai.",
        "en": "Xu Shen's explanation links wu to the word for knight in the Jianghuai dialect."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "black-tortoise-c03",
      "source_id": "black-tortoise-s1",
      "quote": "It represents the north, thus it is sometimes called Black Warrior of the North (北方玄武; Běifāng Xuánwǔ).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xuanwu dikaitkan dengan arah utara.",
        "en": "Xuanwu is associated with the northern direction."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "black-tortoise-c04",
      "source_id": "black-tortoise-s1",
      "quote": "While neither character xuan (玄) or wu (武) literally means turtle, tortoise or snake, tortoises and snakes are known to hibernate during winter, so xuanwu (玄武) as a whole represents the season of winter.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kura-kura dan ular yang berhibernasi mendasari hubungannya dengan musim dingin.",
        "en": "The winter association draws on the hibernation of turtles and snakes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "black-tortoise-c05",
      "source_id": "black-tortoise-s1",
      "quote": "In Japan, the characters xuanwu (玄武) are pronounced as Genbu. It is said to protect Kyoto on the north side, being one of the four guardian spirits that protect the city. It is represented by the Kenkun Shrine, which is located on top of Mount Funaoka in Kyoto.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Jepang, Genbu dipercaya menjaga sisi utara Kyoto.",
        "en": "In Japan, Genbu is believed to guard Kyoto's northern side."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "black-tortoise-c06",
      "source_id": "black-tortoise-s1",
      "quote": "During the Han dynasty, people often wore jade pendants that were in the shape of turtles. Originally, there was a legend that said that turtles could not mate with other turtles, only snakes. This is why the Black Turtle-Snake is depicted as an intertwining turtle and a snake.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah legenda pasangan kura-kura dan ular menjelaskan bentuk mereka yang saling melilit.",
        "en": "A mating legend about turtles and snakes explains their intertwined portrayal."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Artikel pada prompt dan daftar rujukan serta tautan luarnya diperiksa. Rujukan cetak yang belum terbaca dan laman yang tidak mendukung identitas ini tidak dipakai. Sumber pendamping yang belum terverifikasi tetap menjadi kebutuhan riset."

    {

}

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Azure Dragon adalah naga Tiongkok yang termasuk Empat Lambang rasi. Ia mewakili timur dan musim semi.",
      "en": "The Azure Dragon is a Chinese dragon counted among the Four Symbols of the constellations. It corresponds to the eastern direction and spring.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "azure-dragon-c01",
        "azure-dragon-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama Jepang dan Korea untuk naga ini ialah Seiryū dan Cheongryong. Wilayah rasi naga ini mencakup tujuh kedudukan bulan. Azure Dragon juga muncul sebagai dewa pintu di kuil Tao. Lukisan makam Goguryeo di Uhyon-ni menampilkan naga ini bersama lambang lainnya.",
      "en": "Japanese and Korean names for this dragon are Seiryū and Cheongryong. Its constellation region comprises seven lunar mansions. The Azure Dragon also appears as a door deity in Taoist temples. Goguryeo tomb murals at Uhyon-ni depict this dragon with the other symbols.",

      "id": "azure-dragon-s1",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "azure-dragon-c01",
      "source_id": "azure-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The Azure Dragon (simplified Chinese: 青龙; traditional Chinese: 青龍; pinyin: Qīnglóng) is one of the Dragon Gods who represent the mounts or chthonic forces of the Five Regions' Highest Deities (五方上帝; Wǔfāng Shàngdì). It is also one of the Four Symbols of the Chinese constellations, which are the astral representations of the Wufang Shangdi.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Azure Dragon adalah naga Tiongkok yang termasuk Empat Lambang rasi.",
        "en": "The Azure Dragon is a Chinese dragon counted among the Four Symbols of the constellations."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "azure-dragon-c02",
      "source_id": "azure-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The Azure Dragon represents the east and the spring season.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mewakili timur dan musim semi.",
        "en": "It corresponds to the eastern direction and spring."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "azure-dragon-c03",
      "source_id": "azure-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "This dragon is also known as Seiryū in Japanese, Cheongryong in Korean and Thanh Long in Vietnamese.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Jepang dan Korea untuk naga ini ialah Seiryū dan Cheongryong.",
        "en": "Japanese and Korean names for this dragon are Seiryū and Cheongryong."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "azure-dragon-c04",
      "source_id": "azure-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "As with the other three Symbols, there are seven astrological \"Mansions\" (positions of the Moon) within the Azure Dragon. The names and determinative stars are:",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wilayah rasi naga ini mencakup tujuh kedudukan bulan.",
        "en": "Its constellation region comprises seven lunar mansions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "azure-dragon-c05",
      "source_id": "azure-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The Azure Dragon appears as a door god at Taoist temples. He was represented on the tomb of Wang Hui (stone coffin, east side) at Xikang in Lushan. A rubbing of this was collected by David Crockett Graham and is in the Field Museum of Natural History. The dragon featured on the Chinese national flag in 1862–1912, and on the Twelve Symbols national emblem from 1913 to 1928.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Azure Dragon juga muncul sebagai dewa pintu di kuil Tao.",
        "en": "The Azure Dragon also appears as a door deity in Taoist temples."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "azure-dragon-c06",
      "source_id": "azure-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "In Korea, the murals of the Goguryeo tombs found at Uhyon-ni in South Pyongan province features the Azure Dragon and the other mythological creatures of the four symbols.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lukisan makam Goguryeo di Uhyon-ni menampilkan naga ini bersama lambang lainnya.",
        "en": "Goguryeo tomb murals at Uhyon-ni depict this dragon with the other symbols."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Dragon King adalah dewa air dan cuaca yang dikenal dalam tradisi Asia, termasuk agama rakyat Tiongkok.",
    "en": "The Dragon King is a water and weather deity in Asian traditions, including Chinese folk religion.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "dragon-king-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dragon King adalah dewa air dan cuaca yang dikenal dalam tradisi Asia, termasuk agama rakyat Tiongkok. Ia dipercaya mengatur hujan serta menguasai laut, sungai, dan badan air lainnya.",
      "en": "The Dragon King is a water and weather deity in Asian traditions, including Chinese folk religion. Belief attributes rainfall and dominion over seas, rivers, and other waters to him.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dragon-king-c01",
        "dragon-king-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah menggambarkan istana bawah air dan sistem istana kerajaan miliknya. Tradisi juga mengenal Raja Naga Empat Laut. Dewa naga dapat berperan sebagai pelindung wilayah. Desa pertanian dahulu mengadakan upacara di kuil raja naga untuk meminta hujan.",
      "en": "Accounts describe his underwater palace and royal court system. The tradition also recognizes the Dragon Kings of the Four Seas. The dragon deity may also serve as a territorial protector. Farming villages formerly held rites at dragon-king shrines to seek rain.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dragon-king-c03",
        "dragon-king-c04",
        "dragon-king-c05",
        "dragon-king-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],

  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],

    {
      "id": "dragon-king-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dragon_King",
      "title": "Dragon King",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-01"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "dragon-king-c01",
      "source_id": "dragon-king-s1",
      "quote": "The Dragon King, also known as the Dragon God, is a celestial creature, water and weather god in Asian mythology. They can be found in various cultural and religious symbolic materials all around Asia, specifically in South, Southeast Asia and distinctly in East Asian cultures (Chinese folk-religion).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dragon King adalah dewa air dan cuaca yang dikenal dalam tradisi Asia, termasuk agama rakyat Tiongkok.",
        "en": "The Dragon King is a water and weather deity in Asian traditions, including Chinese folk religion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dragon-king-c02",
      "source_id": "dragon-king-s1",
      "quote": "He can manipulate and control the weather, move seasons and bring rainfall with his divine power at his own will, thus, he is regarded as the dispenser of rain, divine ruler of the Seas, rivers and water bodies,  commanding over all bodies of water.  He is the collective personification of the ancient concept of the lóng in Chinese culture and Nāgarāja in Indian culture.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dipercaya mengatur hujan serta menguasai laut, sungai, dan badan air lainnya.",
        "en": "Belief attributes rainfall and dominion over seas, rivers, and other waters to him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dragon-king-c03",
      "source_id": "dragon-king-s1",
      "quote": "It is described that they have their own under-water palace and a royal court system of their own.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah menggambarkan istana bawah air dan sistem istana kerajaan miliknya.",
        "en": "Accounts describe his underwater palace and royal court system."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dragon-king-c04",
      "source_id": "dragon-king-s1",
      "quote": "There are also the cosmological \"Dragon Kings of the Four Seas\" (四海龍王; Sihai Longwang).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi juga mengenal Raja Naga Empat Laut.",
        "en": "The tradition also recognizes the Dragon Kings of the Four Seas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dragon-king-c05",
      "source_id": "dragon-king-s1",
      "quote": "Besides being a water deity, the Dragon God frequently also serves as a territorial tutelary deity, similarly to Tudigong \"Lord of the Earth\" and Houtu \"Queen of the Earth\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dewa naga dapat berperan sebagai pelindung wilayah.",
        "en": "The dragon deity may also serve as a territorial protector."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dragon-king-c06",
      "source_id": "dragon-king-s1",
      "quote": "In the past, there used to be Dragon King miao shrines all over China, for the folk to engage in the worship of dragon kings, villages in farm countries would conduct rites dedicated to the Dragon Kings seeking rain.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Desa pertanian dahulu mengadakan upacara di kuil raja naga untuk meminta hujan.",
        "en": "Farming villages formerly held rites at dragon-king shrines to seek rain."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "Gonggong adalah dewa air dalam mitologi Tiongkok dengan ciri manusia dan ular. Penggambarannya mencakup rambut merah dan dahi besi.",
      "en": "Gonggong is a Chinese water deity portrayed with human and serpentine features. His portrayal includes red hair and an iron forehead.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gonggong-c01",
        "gonggong-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama pribadinya disebut Kanghui. Puisi Tianwen menyalahkannya atas perubahan kemiringan langit dan bumi. Gonggong dikaitkan dengan banjir besar bersama bawahannya Xiangliu. Dalam sebuah mitos, ia merusak Gunung Buzhou setelah kalah melawan Zhurong.",
      "en": "His personal name is given as Kanghui. The Tianwen poem blames him for altering the tilt of sky and earth. Gonggong is linked to great floods alongside his subordinate Xiangliu. In one myth, he damages Mount Buzhou following defeat by Zhurong.",

        "gonggong-c03",

      "source_id": "gonggong-s1",
      "quote": "Gonggong (/ˈɡɒŋɡɒŋ/) is a Chinese water god who is depicted in Chinese mythology and folktales as having a copper human head with an iron forehead, red hair, and the body of a serpent, or sometimes the head and torso are human, with the tail of a serpent. He is destructive and is blamed for various cosmic catastrophes.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gonggong adalah dewa air dalam mitologi Tiongkok dengan ciri manusia dan ular.",
        "en": "Gonggong is a Chinese water deity portrayed with human and serpentine features."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gonggong-c02",
      "source_id": "gonggong-s1",
      "quote": "Gonggong (/ˈɡɒŋɡɒŋ/) is a Chinese water god who is depicted in Chinese mythology and folktales as having a copper human head with an iron forehead, red hair, and the body of a serpent, or sometimes the head and torso are human, with the tail of a serpent. He is destructive and is blamed for various cosmic catastrophes.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambarannya mencakup rambut merah dan dahi besi.",
        "en": "His portrayal includes red hair and an iron forehead."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gonggong-c03",
      "source_id": "gonggong-s1",
      "quote": "Gonggong's personal name is said to be Kanghui (pronounced either /ˈkæŋhuːi/ KANG-hoo-ee in English, or as Mandarin Kānghuí kʰáŋ.xwěi kong-HWAY).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama pribadinya disebut Kanghui.",
        "en": "His personal name is given as Kanghui."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gonggong-c04",
      "source_id": "gonggong-s1",
      "quote": "Gonggong is known from the late Warring States period (before 221 BC). Gonggong appears in the ancient \"Heavenly Questions\" (Tianwen) poem of the Chu Ci, where he is blamed for knocking the Earth's axis off center, causing it to tilt to the southeast and the sky to tilt to the northwest.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Puisi Tianwen menyalahkannya atas perubahan kemiringan langit dan bumi.",
        "en": "The Tianwen poem blames him for altering the tilt of sky and earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gonggong-c05",
      "source_id": "gonggong-s1",
      "quote": "Gonggong was credited in various mythological contexts as being responsible for great floods, often in concert with his minister Xiangliu (a.k.a. Xiangyao), who has nine heads and the body of a snake.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gonggong dikaitkan dengan banjir besar bersama bawahannya Xiangliu.",
        "en": "Gonggong is linked to great floods alongside his subordinate Xiangliu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gonggong-c06",
      "source_id": "gonggong-s1",
      "quote": "Gonggong was ashamed that he lost the fight with Zhurong, the Chinese god of fire, to claim the throne of Heaven. In a fit of rage, he smashed his head against Buzhou Mountain, one of eight pillars holding up the sky, greatly damaging it and causing the sky to tilt towards the northwest and the Earth to shift to the southeast, which caused great floods and suffering.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebuah mitos, ia merusak Gunung Buzhou setelah kalah melawan Zhurong.",
        "en": "In one myth, he damages Mount Buzhou following defeat by Zhurong."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Pixiu adalah makhluk campuran mitologis Tiongkok yang menyerupai singa bersayap. Pixiu dianggap pelindung yang membawa keberuntungan dan kekayaan.",
      "en": "Pixiu are Chinese mythological composite creatures resembling winged lions. Pixiu are regarded as protectors bringing good fortune and wealth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pixiu-c01",
        "pixiu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Salah satu pembagian membedakan Bixie bertanduk dua dan Tianlu bertanduk satu. Patungnya digunakan dalam feng shui untuk menarik kekayaan. Xu Ke menggambarkan pixiu menyerupai harimau atau beruang dengan bulu putih kelabu. Penggambarannya menggabungkan kepala naga Tiongkok dengan tubuh singa.",
      "en": "One division distinguishes two-antlered Bixie from single-antlered Tianlu. Their statues are used in feng shui to attract wealth. Xu Ke describes pixiu as tiger- or bear-like creatures with ashen-white fur. Their portrayal combines a Chinese dragon's head with a lion's body.",
      "claim_ids": [

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "pixiu-c01",
      "source_id": "pixiu-s1",
      "quote": "The pixiu ([pʰǐ.ɕjóʊ]; Chinese: 貔貅) is a Chinese mythical hybrid creature. Pixiu are considered powerful protectors of the souls of the dead, xian, and feng shui practitioners, and resemble strong, winged lions.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pixiu adalah makhluk campuran mitologis Tiongkok yang menyerupai singa bersayap.",
        "en": "Pixiu are Chinese mythological composite creatures resembling winged lions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pixiu-c02",
      "source_id": "pixiu-s1",
      "quote": "The pixiu ([pʰǐ.ɕjóʊ]; Chinese: 貔貅) is a Chinese mythical hybrid creature. Pixiu are considered powerful protectors of the souls of the dead, xian, and feng shui practitioners, and resemble strong, winged lions.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pixiu dianggap pelindung yang membawa keberuntungan dan kekayaan.",
        "en": "Pixiu are regarded as protectors bringing good fortune and wealth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pixiu-c03",
      "source_id": "pixiu-s1",
      "quote": "There are two types of Pixiu that are categorised by their antlers. The one with two antlers is the female and is called a Bìxié, and the one with one antler is the male and is called a Tiānlù.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu pembagian membedakan Bixie bertanduk dua dan Tianlu bertanduk satu.",
        "en": "One division distinguishes two-antlered Bixie from single-antlered Tianlu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pixiu-c04",
      "source_id": "pixiu-s1",
      "quote": "Pixiu crave the smell of gold and silver and like to bring their masters money in their mouth. Statues of this creature are often used to attract wealth in feng shui.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Patungnya digunakan dalam feng shui untuk menarik kekayaan.",
        "en": "Their statues are used in feng shui to attract wealth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pixiu-c05",
      "source_id": "pixiu-s1",
      "quote": "Xu Ke (徐珂) describes the 貔貅; pixiu as resembling either tigers or bears and having ashen white furs, the 貔; pi being male and the 貅; xiu female.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xu Ke menggambarkan pixiu menyerupai harimau atau beruang dengan bulu putih kelabu.",
        "en": "Xu Ke describes pixiu as tiger- or bear-like creatures with ashen-white fur."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pixiu-c06",
      "source_id": "pixiu-s1",
      "quote": "Pixiu are auspicious, winged animals, written about in ancient Chinese history and heralded through the millennia by fantastic stories of powerful and grandiose feats of victory in battle. Their legend has been passed down through 2,000 years of Chinese lore.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambarannya menggabungkan kepala naga Tiongkok dengan tubuh singa.",
        "en": "Their portrayal combines a Chinese dragon's head with a lion's body."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Baku adalah makhluk gaib Jepang yang dipercaya memakan mimpi buruk.",
    "en": "Baku are Japanese supernatural creatures believed to consume nightmares.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "baku-mythology-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Baku adalah makhluk gaib Jepang yang dipercaya memakan mimpi buruk. Asal baku dihubungkan dengan mo Tiongkok dan kisah sisa bahan penciptaan hewan.",
      "en": "Baku are Japanese supernatural creatures believed to consume nightmares. Baku's origin is linked to Chinese mo and a tale about leftover pieces from animal creation.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "baku-mythology-c01",
        "baku-mythology-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Istilah baku juga dipakai untuk tapir Malaya, selain makhluk pemakan mimpi. Baku sudah dikenal di Jepang pada zaman Muromachi. Naskah Sankai Ibutsu menggambarkan belalai serta gading gajah dan telinga badak. Lafcadio Hearn menulis tentang baku pemakan mimpi buruk pada 1902.",
      "en": "The term baku denotes both the dream-eating creature and the Malayan tapir. Baku were known in Japan by the Muromachi period. The Sankai Ibutsu manuscript describes an elephant's trunk and tusks with rhinoceros ears. Lafcadio Hearn wrote about a nightmare-eating baku in 1902.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "baku-mythology-c03",
        "baku-mythology-c04",
        "baku-mythology-c05",
        "baku-mythology-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],
  "timeline": [],
  "relations": [],

  "tradition_vs_modern": null,

  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "baku-mythology-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_(mythology)",
      "title": "Baku (mythology)",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-01"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "baku-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "baku-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Baku (獏 or 貘) are Japanese supernatural beings that are said to devour nightmares. They originate from the Chinese Mo. According to legend, they were created by the spare pieces that were left over when the gods finished creating all other animals. They have a long history in Japanese folklore and art, and more recently have appeared in manga and anime.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Baku adalah makhluk gaib Jepang yang dipercaya memakan mimpi buruk.",
        "en": "Baku are Japanese supernatural creatures believed to consume nightmares."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "baku-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "baku-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Baku (獏 or 貘) are Japanese supernatural beings that are said to devour nightmares. They originate from the Chinese Mo. According to legend, they were created by the spare pieces that were left over when the gods finished creating all other animals. They have a long history in Japanese folklore and art, and more recently have appeared in manga and anime.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asal baku dihubungkan dengan mo Tiongkok dan kisah sisa bahan penciptaan hewan.",
        "en": "Baku's origin is linked to Chinese mo and a tale about leftover pieces from animal creation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "baku-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "baku-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The Japanese term baku has two current meanings, referring to both the traditional dream-devouring creature and to the Malayan tapir.  In recent years, there have been changes in how the baku is depicted.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Istilah baku juga dipakai untuk tapir Malaya, selain makhluk pemakan mimpi.",
        "en": "The term baku denotes both the dream-eating creature and the Malayan tapir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "baku-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "baku-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The traditional Japanese nightmare-devouring baku originates in Chinese folklore from the mo 貘 and was familiar in Japan as early as the Muromachi period (14th–15th century). Hori Tadao has described the dream-eating abilities attributed to the traditional baku and relates them to other preventatives against nightmare such as amulets.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Baku sudah dikenal di Jepang pada zaman Muromachi.",
        "en": "Baku were known in Japan by the Muromachi period."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "baku-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "baku-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Before its adaptation to the Japanese dream-caretaker myth creature, an early 17th-century Japanese manuscript, the Sankai Ibutsu (山海異物), describes the baku as a shy, Chinese mythical chimera with the trunk and tusks of an elephant, the ears of a rhinoceros, the tail of a cow, the body of a bear and the paws of a tiger, which protected against pestilence and evil, although eating nightma",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naskah Sankai Ibutsu menggambarkan belalai serta gading gajah dan telinga badak.",
        "en": "The Sankai Ibutsu manuscript describes an elephant's trunk and tusks with rhinoceros ears."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "baku-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "baku-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Writing in the Meiji period, Lafcadio Hearn (1902) described a baku with very similar attributes that was also able to devour nightmares.\nLegend has it that a person who wakes up from a bad dream can call out to baku.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lafcadio Hearn menulis tentang baku pemakan mimpi buruk pada 1902.",
        "en": "Lafcadio Hearn wrote about a nightmare-eating baku in 1902."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Dalam percakapan Korea, gumiho juga dapat menyebut orang yang dianggap licik. Tidak semua roh rubah dalam tradisi Korea memiliki sembilan ekor.       "quote": "A gumiho or kumiho (Korean: 구미호; Hanja: 九尾狐; lit. 'nine-tailed fox') is a creature that appears in the folktales of East Asia and legends of Korea. It is similar to the Chinese jiuweihu, the Japanese kitsune and the Vietnamese hồ ly tinh.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gumiho adalah rubah berekor sembilan dalam cerita dan legenda Korea.",
        "en": "Gumiho is a nine-tailed fox in Korean tales and legends."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gumiho-c02",
      "source_id": "gumiho-s1",
      "quote": "Gumiho is a term that originally referred to a magical fox appearing in Korean novels of the Joseon dynasty, or was used pejoratively in historical records of the same period to denounce treacherous officials. In modern Korea, the designation gumiho has been broadened to encompass all fox spirits described in traditional Korean sources.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebutan ini digunakan untuk rubah ajaib dalam novel zaman Joseon.",
        "en": "The designation was used for magical foxes in Joseon-era novels."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gumiho-c03",
      "source_id": "gumiho-s1",
      "quote": "In Korean colloquial usage, gumiho is often applied to describe a person (especially a woman) regarded as sly or cunning.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam percakapan Korea, gumiho juga dapat menyebut orang yang dianggap licik.",
        "en": "In colloquial Korean, gumiho may describe someone regarded as cunning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gumiho-c04",
      "source_id": "gumiho-s1",
      "quote": "Due to the widespread use of the term gumiho, modern Koreans often refer to all fox spirits by this name; however, this represents a typical case of conflation.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tidak semua roh rubah dalam tradisi Korea memiliki sembilan ekor.",
        "en": "Korean tradition includes fox spirits beyond the nine-tailed type."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gumiho-c05",
      "source_id": "gumiho-s1",
      "quote": "In Korean myth, foxes are primarily depicted as deceptive, malevolent, and tragic beings, though there are rare cases where they are portrayed as benevolent toward humans or as deities governing mountains.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah biasanya menggambarkan rubah sebagai penipu atau makhluk tragis, meski ada varian baik.",
        "en": "Tales usually portray foxes as deceptive or tragic, though benevolent variants exist."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gumiho-c06",
      "source_id": "gumiho-s1",
      "quote": "They are often portrayed in the form of heretical Buddhist monks or beautiful women and are sometimes depicted as monsters that torment and threaten dragons, which are considered sacred in Korean mythology.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh rubah sering mengambil rupa perempuan cantik atau biksu sesat.",
        "en": "Fox spirits often appear as beautiful women or heretical monks."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Huli jing adalah roh rubah dalam mitologi Tiongkok yang dapat berubah wujud. Roh rubah dapat bersifat baik atau jahat.",
      "en": "Huli jing are shapeshifting fox spirits of Chinese mythology. Fox spirits may be benevolent or malevolent.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "huli-jing-c01",
        "huli-jing-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kehadirannya dapat menjadi pertanda baik maupun buruk, bergantung pada kisah. Shanhaijing memuat penyebutan rubah berekor sembilan. Guo Pu menafsirkan rubah berekor sembilan sebagai pertanda kedamaian. Versi dari Gunung Qingqiu menggambarkannya bersuara bayi dan memakan manusia.",
      "en": "Their presence may be a favorable or adverse omen depending on the tale. The Shanhaijing contains references to nine-tailed foxes. Guo Pu interprets the nine-tailed fox as an auspicious sign of peace. The Qingqiu Mountain account describes an infant-like cry and man-eating behavior.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "huli-jing-c03",
        "huli-jing-c04",
        "huli-jing-c05",
        "huli-jing-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
